SECTION B - CONTINUATION OF SF 1449 BLOCKS 
 
B.1 GOVERNING LAW 
 
Federal law and regulations, including the Federal Acquisition Regulations (FAR), shall govern 
this Contract/Order. Commercial license agreements may be made a part of this Contract/Order 
but only if both parties expressly make them an addendum hereto.  If the commercial license 
agreement is not made an addendum, it shall not apply, govern, be a part of or have any effect 
whatsoever on the Contract/Order; this includes, but is not limited to, any agreement embedded 
in the computer software (clickwrap), any agreement that is otherwise delivered with or provided 
to the Government with the commercial computer software or documentation (shrinkwrap), or 
any other license agreement otherwise referred to in any document.  If a commercial license 
agreement is made an addendum, only those provisions addressing data rights regarding the 
Government’s use, duplication and disclosure of data (e.g., restricted computer software) are 
included and made a part of this Contract/Order, and only to the extent that those provisions are 
not duplicative or inconsistent with Federal law, Federal regulation, the incorporated FAR 
clauses and the provisions of this Contract/Order; those provisions in the commercial license 
agreement that do not address data rights regarding the Government’s use, duplication and 
disclosure of data shall not be included or made a part of the Contract/Order. Federal law and 
regulation including, without limitation, the Contract Disputes Act (41 U.S.C. § 7101 et seq.), 
the Anti-Deficiency Act (31 U.S.C. § 1341 et seq.), the Competition in Contracting Act (41 
U.S.C. § 3301 et seq.), the Prompt Payment Act (31 U.S.C. § 3901 et seq.), Contracts for Data 
Processing or Maintenance (38 USC § 5725), Source Code Harmonization and Reuse in 
Information Technology Act “SHARE IT Act” (P.L. 118-187), and FAR clauses 52.212-4, 
52.227-14 shall supersede, control, and render ineffective any inconsistent, conflicting, or 
duplicative provision in any commercial license agreement.  In the event of conflict between this 
clause and any provision in the Contract/Order or the commercial license agreement or 
elsewhere, the terms of this clause shall prevail.  The Contractor shall deliver to the Government 
all data first produced under this Contract/Order with unlimited rights as defined by FAR 
52.227-14, this includes any custom code developed for the purpose of performing under this 
contract.  Claims of patent or copyright infringement brought against the Government as a party 
shall be defended by the U.S. Department of Justice (DOJ) in accordance with 28 U.S.C. § 516; 
at the discretion of DOJ, the Contractor may be allowed reasonable participation in the defense 
of the litigation. Any additional changes to the Contract/Order must be made by modification 
(Standard Form 30) and shall only be made by a warranted Contracting Officer.  Nothing in this 
Contract/Order or any commercial license agreement shall be construed as a waiver of sovereign 
immunity.

B.2 SOFTWARE LICENSE, MAINTENANCE AND TECHNICAL SUPPORT  
(1). Definitions. 
 
a) Licensee.  The term “licensee” shall mean the U.S. Department of Veterans Affairs 
(“VA”) and is synonymous with “Government.”  
b) Licensor.  The term “licensor” shall mean the Contractor having the necessary license or 
ownership rights to deliver license, software maintenance and support of the computer software 
being acquired.  The term “Contractor” is the party identified in Block 17a on the SF1449.  If the 
Contractor is a reseller and not the Licensor, the Contractor remains responsible for performance 
under this Contract/Order.  
c) Software.  The term “software” shall mean the licensed computer software product(s) 
cited in the Schedule of Supplies/Services.  
d) Maintenance.  The term “maintenance” is the process of enhancing and optimizing 
software, as well as remedying defects.  It shall include all new fixes, patches, releases, updates, 
versions and upgrades, as further defined below.  
e) Technical Support.  The term “technical support” refers to the range of services providing 
assistance for the software via the telephone, email, a website or otherwise.  
f) Release or Update.  The term “release” or “update” are terms that refer to a revision of 
software that contains defect corrections, minor enhancements or improvements of the software’s 
functionality.  This is usually designated by a change in the number to the right of the decimal 
point (e.g., from Version 5.3 to 5.4).  An example of an update is the addition of new hardware.  
g) Version or Upgrade.  The term “version” or “upgrade” are terms that refer to a revision of 
software that contains new or improved functionality.  This is usually designated by a change in 
the number to the left of the decimal point (e.g., from Version 5.4 to 6).  
 
(2). Software License. 
 
a) Unless otherwise stated in the Schedule of Supplies/Services, the Performance Work 
Statement or Product Description, the software license provided to the Government is a 
perpetual, nonexclusive license to use the software.  
b) The Government may use the software in a networked environment.  
c) Any dispute regarding the license grant or usage limitations shall be resolved in 
accordance with the Disputes Clause incorporated in FAR 52.212-4(d).  
d) All limitations of software usage are expressly stated in the Schedule of 
Supplies/Services and the Performance Work Statement/Product Description.  
 
(3). Software Maintenance and Technical Support. 
 
a) If the Government desires to continue software maintenance and support beyond the 
period of performance identified in this Contract/Order, the Government will issue a separate 
contract or order for maintenance and support.  Conversely, if a contract or order for continuing 
software maintenance and technical support is not received, the Contractor is neither authorized 
nor permitted to renew any of the previously furnished services.  
b) The Contractor shall provide software support services, which includes periodic updates, 
enhancements and corrections to the software, and reasonable technical support, all of which are 
customarily provided by the Contractor to its commercial customers so as to cause the software 
to perform according to its specifications, documentation or demonstrated claims.  
c) Any telephone support provided by Contractor shall be at no additional cost.  

--- PAGE 5 ---
36C10B26R0048 
 
 
Page 5 of 153 
d) The Contractor shall provide all maintenance services in a timely manner in accordance 
with the Contractor’s customary practice or as defined in the Performance Work Statement or 
Product Description.  However, prolonged delay (exceeding 2 business days) in resolving 
software problems will be noted in the Government’s various past performance records on the 
Contractor (e.g., www.ppirs.gov).  
e) If the Government allows the maintenance and support to lapse and subsequently wishes 
to reinstate it, any reinstatement fee charged shall not exceed the amounts that would have been 
charged if the Government had not allowed the subscription to lapse.  
 
(4). Disabling Software Code.  The Government requires delivery of computer software that does 
not contain any code that will, upon the occurrence or the nonoccurrence of any event, disable 
the software.  Such code includes but is not limited to a computer virus, restrictive key, node 
lock, time-out or other function, whether implemented by electronic, mechanical, or other means, 
which limits or hinders the use or access to any computer software based on residency on a 
specific hardware configuration, frequency of duration of use, or other limiting criteria.  If any 
such disabling code is present, the Contractor agrees to indemnify the Government for all 
damages suffered as a result of a disabling caused by such code, and the contractor agrees to 
remove such code upon the Government’s request at no extra cost to the Government.  Inability 
of the Contractor to remove the disabling software code will be considered an inexcusable delay 
and a material breach of contract, and the Government may exercise its right to terminate for 
cause.  In addition, the Government is permitted to remove the code as it deems appropriate and 
charge the Contractor for consideration for the time and effort expended in removing the code. 
 
(5). Manuals and Publications.  Upon Government request, the Contractor shall furnish the most 
current version of the user manual and publications for all products/services provided under this 
Contract/Order at no cost.   
 
(6). Data Rights.  
 
a.     Notwithstanding any contrary provisions contained in the Contractor’s standard commercial 
license, the Contractor agrees that the Government will have the rights that are set forth 
in paragraph (b) below to use, duplicate or disclose any commercial computer software delivered 
under this Contract/Order.    
 
b. The commercial computer software delivered under this Contract/Order may not be used, 
reproduced, or disclosed by the Government except as provided below or as 
expressly stated otherwise in this Contract/Order.  The commercial computer 
software may be-  
• Used or copied for use with the computer(s) for which it was acquired, including 
use at any Government installation to which the computer(s) may be transferred;  
• Used or copied for use with a backup computer if any computer for which it 
was acquired is inoperative;  
• Reproduced for safekeeping (archives) or backup purposes;  
• Modified, adapted, or combined with other computer software, provided that the 
modified, adapted, or combined portions of the derivative software incorporating 
any of the delivered, commercial computer software shall be subject to same 
restrictions set forth in this Contract/Order;  

--- PAGE 6 ---
36C10B26R0048 
 
 
Page 6 of 153 
• Disclosed to and reproduced for use by support service Contractors or their 
subcontractors, subject to the same restrictions set forth in this Contract/Order; 
and  
• Used or copied for use with a replacement computer.  
• If the commercial computer software is otherwise available without disclosure 
restrictions, the Contractor licenses it to the Government without disclosure 
restrictions.  The terms and provisions of this Contract/Order shall comply 
with Federal laws and the Federal Acquisition Regulation.  
 
c.  The Contractor shall affix a notice substantially as follows to any commercial computer 
software delivered under this Contract/Order: “Notice-Notwithstanding any other license 
agreement that may pertain to, or accompany the delivery of, this computer software, the 
rights of the Government regarding its use, reproduction and disclosure are as set forth 
in Government Contract No. ______________________.”

B.4 PRICE SCHEDULE 
Deliverables shall be provided electronic submission to the VA Program Manager (PM) and the  
Contracting Officer Representative (CORS).  
 
The resulting contract will be awarded on a firm-fixed-price basis as defined by Federal 
Acquisition Regulation Subpart 16.202.  Accordingly, the Contractor shall ensure that any and 
all costs associated with the Contractor’s proposed application(s), software products, software 
solution, and/or system, shall be included in the Contractor’s proposed firm-fixed price, and shall 
serve as the Contractor’s firm-fixed price for the life of any resulting contract.  No additional 
costs or fees relative to the Contractor’s proposed application(s), software products, software 
solution, and/or system including, but not limited to, licensing costs and any associated licensing 
maintenance required for the development, delivery, integration, operation, and/or maintenance 
of the Contractor’s proposed solution will be allowed, accepted, and/or paid by the Government. 
 
Days used below refer to calendar days unless otherwise stated. Deliverables with due dates 
falling on a weekend or holiday shall be submitted the following Government workday after the 
weekend or holiday. 
 
Electronic submission to: VA Program Manager, Contracting Officer’s Representatives and 
Contracting Officer

Michelle Prinston, michelle.prinston@va.gov and Contracting Officer, Jason King,  
Jason.king6@va.gov prior to the closing date and time specified.

B.5 PERFORMANCE WORK STATEMENT 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
PERFORMANCE WORK STATEMENT (PWS)  
DEPARTMENT OF VETERANS AFFAIRS  
 
Office of Information & Technology (OIT) 
Product Delivery Service (PDS) Health Services Portfolio (HSP) 
Healthcare Environment and Logistics Management (HELM) 
 
 
Supply Chain Management Development Security Operations (SCMDSO) Integration 
 
 
Date: June 30, 2026  
VA-26-00021946 
 
PWS Version Number: 1.0 
 
  


--- PAGE 32 ---
36C10B26R0048 
 
 
Page 32 of 153 
1.0 BACKGROUND 
The Department of Veterans Affairs (VA) Office of Information and Technology (OIT) Product 
Delivery Services (PDS) delivers integrated digital capabilities that support Veteran care 
nationwide.  Within PDS, the Health Services Portfolio’s (HSP) Healthcare Environment and 
Logistics Management (HELM) Product Line (PL) implements, modernizes, sustains, 
standardizes, and streamlines software solutions for supply chain, logistics, expendables, assets, 
inventory, facilities, prosthetics, and environmental compliance. HELM provides the enterprise 
capabilities needed to support a unified, efficient, and data driven supply chain and logistics 
environment across VA. 
 
VA does not currently operate with standardized, enterprise-wide supply chain business 
processes or technical systems. Numerous functional and capability gaps have been identified 
through Government Accountability Office (GAO) and Office of Inspector General (OIG) audits 
and internal assessments.  VA requires accurate, reliable, and accessible supply chain data to 
improve ordering, fulfillment, and operational performance.  These needs align with VA’s 
Strategic Supply Chain (SC) Prioritization and Digital Modernization Strategy, which emphasize 
modernizing legacy systems, strengthening cybersecurity and resilience, improving data quality, 
enhancing user experience for logisticians, and establishing consistent, interoperable processes 
across Veterans Health Administration (VHA), VA Central Office (VACO), Veterans Benefit 
Administration (VBA), and National Cemetery Administration (NCA). 
 
VA currently relies on approximately 63 disparate legacy systems across 174 sites, resulting in 
fragmented operations, limited enterprise visibility, and inefficiencies. Standardization and 
modernization are required to transform SC systems into integrated, enterprise-wide system of 
systems that supports responsive, efficient clinical and nonclinical supply operations. 
 
To meet mission needs, VA requires a full service, standardized, scalable healthcare logistics 
software solutions with modern supply chain management capabilities.  A unified digital 
environment shall enable lifecycle management efficiencies, data driven decision making, and 
seamless integration with major initiatives such as the Electronic Health Record Modernization 
(EHRM) and Financial Management Business Transformation (FMBT) programs.  HELM’s 
mission and modernization efforts directly support these enterprise requirements by advancing 
cloud-based logistics capabilities, modernizing legacy applications, and enabling consistent 
processes across VA’s supply chain functions.  In addition to modernization activities, several 
HELM products require ongoing sustainment support, enhancements, development, operation 
stability, security and Lifecyle support.  
 
HELM is organized into five Sub Product Lines (SPL): Environmental and Healthcare 
Management, Healthcare Technology and Asset Management, Expendable Inventory 
Management, Prosthetics, and Technical Services. Additional products such as Enterprise Asset 
Management-Computerized Maintenance Management System (EAM-CMMS) and Enterprise 
Expendable Inventory Management (EEIM) are scheduled to be incorporated into the PL over 
the course of this task order Period of Performance (PoP), see Attachment 1, HELM Products.  
Attachment 1 represents both products that HELM provides full Software Development Life 
Cycle (SDLC) support and ownership, and VHA owned products where HELM provides varying 
levels of support.  
 
To ensure continuity of critical HELM Product Line services throughout modernization and 
mandatory sustainment activities Optional Tasks may be exercised  to maintain mandatory 

--- PAGE 33 ---
36C10B26R0048 
 
 
Page 33 of 153 
service levels and prevent disruption to ongoing development, integration, and Operations and 
Maintenance (O&M) activities. Additionally, the Contractor shall maintain all required artifacts 
in a complete and current state to support seamless transition between contract teams, ensuring 
VA retains full operational continuity and institutional knowledge throughout the PoP. 
VA employs a DevSecOps methodology integrated with Agile practices, emphasizing 
continuous delivery, iterative improvement, and security as a foundational element of the 
development lifecycle, not an afterthought. This approach reflects VA's commitment to 
delivering value rapidly and responsibly: getting new capabilities into the hands of Veterans and 
care teams quickly, while maintaining the highest standards of security and integrity for sensitive 
health information protected under Federal law. 
The requirements set forth in this PWS provide comprehensive development, testing, 
deployment, operations, maintenance, and security support across legacy, current, and future 
HELM PL products and services throughout the full DevSecOps lifecycle.

3.0 SCOPE OF WORK 
The Contractor shall provide comprehensive technical, functional, and lifecycle support for 
existing legacy and future HELM PL products, and new systems incorporated into the product 
line, in alignment with VA’s enterprise digital modernization, cybersecurity, and supply chain 
transformation strategies.  This support shall enable VA’s strategic objectives to modernize 
platforms, standardize, enhance interoperability, strengthen cybersecurity posture, improve 
supply chain visibility, and deliver resilient and pleasant user experience in all modern systems.  
.Products may be modernized through replacement or incremental modernization.  When they 
are modernized, the corresponding legacy systems will be decommissioned. The Government 
will provide necessary requirements, system access, and stakeholder coordination.  The 
Contractor shall support all HELM products, including—but not limited to—Inventory 
Management, Financial Management, Asset Management, other Supply Chain systems, 
Prosthetics, VistA modules such as MUMPS-based components, and Nuvolo/ServiceNow. 
 
The Contractor shall provide full SDLC services; sustainment, modernization, enhancements, 
and development; configuration; cybersecurity and regulatory compliance; enterprise 
deployment; incremental enhancements; training; software download & installation, 
implementation and integration support; database administration; interface development; 
application user account management, platform management; testing, Cloud Migration and 
Hosting services consistent with internal VA Enterprise Cloud (VAEC) standards or external 
Platform as a Service (PaaS) and/or external Infrastructure as a Service (IaaS) Federal standards; 
and modernization activities supporting HELM PL managed solutions.  The Contractor shall 
employ lean scaled agile frameworks, such as the Scaled Agile Framework (SAFe) and other 
emerging innovative frameworks, to deliver a broad range of solutions across the PL. 
 
The Contractor shall apply advanced DevSecOps and engineering capabilities aligned with VA’s 
Zero Trust Architecture (ZTA) Strategy, VA Digital Modernization Strategy, and SC 
modernization objectives, and future quantum computing considerations.  Required activities 
include secure software development.  Continuous Integration and Continuous Delivery (CI/CD) 
automation, pipeline optimization, continuous monitoring, security assessment and authorization 
support, configuration and release management, architecture and interoperability analysis, 
training, data validation and analytics, supply chain risk assessment, and development of 
technical roadmaps and recommendations that advance VA’s enterprise modernization goals. 
 
The Contractor shall also provide program management, technical governance, intake and 
prioritization support, business process analysis, Risk Management Framework activities, Agile 
delivery management, and required documentation to ensure secure, efficient, and compliant 
delivery of PL capabilities.  All services shall adhere to VA cybersecurity, privacy, Section 508, 
and quality standards and shall integrate with VA enterprise platforms and shared services. All 
work shall comply with applicable VA security, privacy, and quality standards and shall align 
with OIT HELM PL’s mission to modernize, secure, streamline, and sustain VA SC and logistics 
capabilities.   
3.1 ORDER TYPE 
This effort shall be a hybrid Firm-Fixed Price (FFP) Task Order with a Time and Materials 
(T&M) line for Travel.

4.0 PERFORMANCE DETAILS 
4.1 PERFORMANCE PERIOD 
The PoP shall be 3-month base period with four 12-month option periods, one nine-month option 
period, and three optional tasks, if exercised.  The overall PoP shall not exceed 66 months. 
Base period shall be one three – month period and shall consist of activities described in PWS 
paragraphs: 5.1.3; 5.1.4; 5.1.5; 5.2.1; 5.2.2; 5.2.2.1. 
 
Any work at the Government site shall not take place on Federal holidays or weekends unless 
directed by the Contracting Officer (CO).  If required, the CO or Contracting Officer’s 
Representatives (CORs) or may direct the Contractor to work during holidays and weekends.  
 
There are 11 Federal holidays set by law (USC Title 5 Section 6103) that VA follows: 
 
Under current definitions, five are set by date: 
 
New Year's Day  January 1 
Juneteenth   June 19 
Independence Day  July 4 
Veterans Day   November 11 
Christmas Day   December 25 
 
If any of the above falls on a Saturday, then Friday shall be observed as a holiday.  Similarly, if 
one falls on a Sunday, then Monday shall be observed as a holiday. 
 
The other six are set by a day of the week and month: 
 
Martin Luther King's Birthday Third Monday in January 
Washington's Birthday Third Monday in February 
Memorial Day Last Monday in May 
Labor Day First Monday in September 
Columbus Day Second Monday in October 
Thanksgiving Fourth Thursday in November  
4.2 PLACE OF PERFORMANCE 
Tasks under this PWS shall be performed at Contractor facilities.  The Contractor shall identify 
the Contractors place of performance in their proposal submission.  Work may be performed at 
remote locations with prior concurrence from the CORs 
 
When travel is required, work at the Government site may be required to take place on holidays, 
or weekends or after business hours and will be directed by CO or the CORs.  Travel to a 
Government facility needed to perform the services doesn’t change the Place of Performance to 
the Government Site. 
 
4.3 TRAVEL 
The Government encourages maximum utilization of teleconferencing and video conferencing 
for meetings.

substitution must possess equal or greater qualifications, experience, and certifications to

5.0 SPECIFIC TASKS AND DELIVERABLES 
The Contractor shall perform the following tasks and provide the specific deliverables described 
below for each product/project initiated within the PoP stated in this PWS. 
 
The Contractor and/or its subcontractors shall be required, as part of performance under this 
Task Order, to work with other Government Contractors.  In performance of this task order the 
Contractor shall provide full cooperation with all contractors working for and on behalf of the 
VA.  The Contractor is not authorized to provide guidance, direction, and /or approval of any 
Contractor’s work activities, work products, or deliverables; VA staff will provide all directions 
and/or approval.  
 
Upon award, the CORs will establish initial contact between the Contractor and other 
Contractors.  All interested parties shall participate as applicable.  Nondisclosure forms between 
VA and Contractors shall be executed as appropriate.  
.   
The Contractor shall utilize the SDLC and apply the Agile framework for product management 
to deliver iteratively and incrementally, consistently producing high quality results that are cost 
effective, timely, and achieved through a highly collaborative manner. 

--- PAGE 42 ---
36C10B26R0048 
 
 
Page 42 of 153 
The Contractor shall utilize VA TRM compliant tools in performance of Agile Product 
Management, test results, scenarios, and results for each product.  
 
The Contractor shall adhere to VA policies by ensuring compliance with DevSecOps practices, 
implementing Agile methodologies, and supporting other OIT strategic initiatives.  To achieve 
these requirements, the Contractor shall provide a team of subject matter experts (SME) who will 
facilitate the integration and oversight of capabilities at both the product line and the  
product/solution levels, working collaboratively with stakeholders and partners.   
 
All tasks and deliverables described in Sections 5.0 through 5.13 shall be executed using an 
iterative Agile delivery model performed through recurring, timeboxed two-week sprints, unless 
otherwise approved by the CORs.  Each sprint shall include Sprint Planning, Daily Scrum 
meetings, backlog refinement, build and integration activities, continuous testing, a Sprint 
Review, and a Sprint Retrospective.  The Contractor shall ensure that all development, 
sustainment, DevSecOps, testing, data management, architecture, release preparation, 
documentation, and operational readiness activities are delivered incrementally within this sprint 
cadence. 
 
The Contractor shall maintain all sprint artifacts including Sprint Plans, Sprint Backlogs, 
working software increments, test evidence, configuration items, defects, risks, impediments, and 
traceability data within VA approved Agile lifecycle management tools.  These tools serve as the 
authoritative source for documenting progress, demonstrating completion of acceptance criteria, 
and supporting Product Line governance 
 
VA will follow an intake process outlined in PWS Section 5.2.2 when new or existing 
products/applications/systems require support services from the task order.   
5.1 PROJECT MANAGEMENT  
 
5.1.1 CONTRACTOR PROJECT MANAGEMENT PLAN 
The Contractor shall deliver a Contractor Project Management Plan (CPMP) that lays out the 
Contractor’s approach, timeline, and tools to be used in execution of the task order.  The CPMP 
should take the form of both a narrative and graphic format that displays the schedule, 
milestones, risks, and resource support.  The CPMP shall also include how the Contractor shall 
coordinate and execute planned, routine, and ad hoc data collection reporting requests as 
identified within the PWS.  The initial baseline CPMP shall be provided within one month of the 
award to the VA Product Manager/CORs who will approve/concur.  The Contractor shall update 
and maintain the approved CPMP throughout the PoP.  The updated CPMP shall be provided 
monthly, or as needed, to the VA Product Manager/CORs.  The Contractor shall provide a 
contractor functional organization chart to ensure contractor leads along with SMEs are clearly 
delineated.

5.1.4 TECHNICAL KICKOFF MEETING 
A technical kickoff meeting shall be held within 10 days after award.  The Contractor shall 
coordinate the date, time, and location (can be virtual) with the CO, the VA PM, the Contract 
Specialist (CS), and the CORs.  The Contractor shall provide a draft agenda to the CO, VA PM, 
and CORs at least five calendar days prior to the meeting.  Upon Government approval of the 
final agenda, the Contractor shall distribute to all meeting attendees.  During the kickoff meeting, 
the Contractor shall present, for review and approval by the Government, the details of the 
intended approach, work plan, and project schedule for each effort via a Microsoft Office 
PowerPoint presentation.  At the conclusion of the meeting, the Contractor shall update the 
presentation with a final slide entitled “Summary Report” which shall include notes on any major 
issues, agreements, or disagreements discussed during the kickoff meeting.  The Contractor shall 
distribute the updated presentation and meeting minutes to the CO, VA PM, CORs and all 
attendees within three calendar days after the meeting. 
 
Deliverable: 
A. Kickoff Meeting Agenda, Kickoff Meeting Minutes and Final presentation  
 
5.1.5 ONBOARDING / OFFBOARDING 
The Contractor shall support the onboarding and offboarding of its staff.  The Contractor shall 
provide a task order lead to support onboarding and offboarding actions in the Identity and 
Access Management (IAM) Portal, to include TMS training.   A single Contractor Onboarding 
Point of Contact (POC) and back up POC shall be designated by the Contractor that tracks the 
onboarding, offboarding, and training status of all Contractor personnel.  The Contractor shall 
maintain a Master Onboarding Tracking Document as described in section 5.1.5.4, and provided 
monthly to the CORs, VA PM or Government designee.  The Contractor shall provide a Weekly 
Onboarding/Offboarding Status Report as described in sections 5.1.5.1 and 5.1.5.2.  The format 
for the weekly onboarding and offboarding status reports shall be developed collaboratively and 
approved by the CORs and the Contractor which shall be saved with all other deliverables.  The 
Contractor shall provide an onboarding status in the Weekly Onboarding/Offboarding Status 
Report to the CORs, VA PM or Government designee. 
 
5.1.5.1 Onboarding Activities 
Onboarding includes steps to obtain a VA PIV card, network and email account, complete 
training, initiate background investigations, and obtain Government Furnished Equipment 
(GFE).  In addition, the Contractor shall identify individuals which may require elevated 
privileges to the necessary development and test environments for the various systems to be 
developed or enhanced.   
The Contractor Onboarding POCs shall be responsible for accurate and timely submission of all 
required VA onboarding paperwork to the VA CORs or applicable systems used for onboarding.  
The Contractor shall be responsible for tracking the status of all its staff’s onboarding activities 

--- PAGE 45 ---
36C10B26R0048 
 
 
Page 45 of 153 
to include the names of all personnel engaged on the task, commercial email address, phone 
numbers, immediate task order supervisor, onboarding milestone dates, and their initial training 
date for VA Privacy and Information Security training.  The Weekly Onboarding/Offboarding 
Status Report shall include: 
 
• Current onboarding status for all personnel. 
• Pending onboarding actions. 
• Estimated completion dates for outstanding steps. 
• Issues or delays impacting onboarding timelines.  
 
5.1.5.2 Offboarding Activities 
The Contractor shall ensure timely offboarding of personnel, including: 
• Return of GFE. 
• Revocation of elevated privileges. 
• Deactivation of network and email accounts. 
• Notification to CORs of personnel departure. 
 
The Contractor shall provide an offboarding status in the Weekly Onboarding/Offboarding 
Status Report, detailing the status of all offboarding activities.   
 
5.1.5.3 VA Mandatory Training 
All resources are required to have access to the VA network and shall complete all the required 
VA Talent Management System (TMS) training courses within three business days of submitting 
an onboarding package.  The Contractor shall work with its respective onboarding point of 
contact and CORs to obtain access to TMS to complete the mandatory training courses. 
 
As an action under the Continuous Readiness in Information Security Program, VA's Assistant 
Secretary for Information and Technology issued a memorandum requiring all VA Government 
and task order staff to complete information security awareness and applicable role-based 
training.  The Contractor shall ensure Training completion and track TMS Training Certificates 
for VA Privacy and Information Security Awareness, Rules of Behavior, Health Insurance 
Portability and Accountability Act (HIPAA).  If elevated privileges are required, Role-Based 
training shall be identified for completion prior to gaining additional access. 
 
TMS training status shall be included in the Master Onboarding Tracking Document.

5.2 HELM PRODUCT LINE MANAGEMENT SUPPORT  
 
5.2.1 HELM PRODUCT LINE SUPPORT 
The Contractor shall holistically advance the systems engineering maturity of the HELM PL by 
ensuring compliance with DevSecOps practices, implementing Agile methodologies, and 
supporting VA’s Strategic Supply Chain Prioritization and Digital Modernization Strategy.  To 
achieve this, the Contractor shall provide a qualified team of SMEs to facilitate the integration 
and oversight of capabilities & practices at both the product line and value stream levels, 
working collaboratively with stakeholders and partners.  The Contractor shall support 
development of HELM PL briefing slides to include monthly Program Management Reviews 
(PMR) and Program Increment (PI) Planning committed and uncommitted objectives.  The 
Contractor shall provide cross-functional SAFe resources to include SC Technical Expertise for 
the development of enhancements to the HELM products and underlying infrastructure in 
alignment with VA policies and procedures. 
 
The Contractor shall: 
 
• Track, process and manage Authority to Operate (ATO) activities for PL by working with 
each product and SMEs to ensure compliance. 
• Provide technical leads/SAFe Roles for PL management for IT Program Manager Lead, 
Enterprise/Solution System Architect, Engineer Solution Lead, Data Architect Lead, Test 
Automation Lead, Solution Technical Lead , Cyber Security Architect /Policy Lead 
• Assist in defining new application implementation requirements, to include the 
generation of Epics and translation to Features and User Stories which make up the 
Product Roadmap and Product Increment Plans while mitigating dependencies between 
them.  
• Consolidate requirements or sequencing to generate product line efficiencies  
• Understand future state requirements for the successful implementation of new 
applications in VA and identify impacts to legacy or dependent systems.  
• Provide guidance on dependencies between implementation requirements within and 
outside the product to reduce risk as new capabilities are introduced and current systems 
are retired.  
• Identify and analyze the systems targeted for integration and the design and technologies 
needed to implement them.  
• Assist with database solutions and designs for data migrations for implementations at VA 
sites.  
• Provide product line analyses of risks and issues to identify trends and possible 
opportunities for responses and mitigations.  
• Understand current and future-state data flows, networking, cybersecurity, and 
infrastructure requirements. 
• Conduct testing, training, and sustainment  
 
 
5.2.2 REQUIREMENT ANALYSIS PROPOSAL INTAKE PROCESS  
HELM PL requires a standardized, repeatable, and transparent intake process to manage 
Government provided requirements for existing products, ongoing work, and any new 
capabilities introduced during the PoP.  The Contractor shall support the Government to mature, 

--- PAGE 47 ---
36C10B26R0048 
 
 
Page 47 of 153 
operate, and continuously improve the existing HELM intake process, ensuring all requests are 
evaluated consistently and accurately, and in alignment with established prioritization criteria.   
 
Applicable deliverables will be assigned during Requirements Analysis Proposal (RAP) process 
to ensure traceability, visibility and alignment with government expectations.  The Contractor 
shall ensure that all intake artifacts and related workflow outputs are accessible to the 
Government and maintained in accordance with VA standards for configuration management, 
transparency, and auditability. 
 
The Contractor shall: 
• Develop and maintain intake Standard Operating Procedures (SOPs), including required 
data elements, routing logic, approval steps, timelines, artifacts, workflows, forms, 
estimation model, and architecture reuse guidance. 
• Ensure SOPs reflect the actual tools and processes used by HELM PL teams. 
• Provide updates to the Intake Analysis and SOP, as needed or as directed, by HELM 
governance. 
 
The Contractor shall not create a new custom-made intake application or standalone web portal.  
Instead, the Contractor shall use and enhance VA approved platforms (e.g., Jira, SharePoint, 
Confluence, Power Apps/Automate, or other approved tools) to support intake activities, 
workflow routing, estimation, documentation, and reporting.   
 
Within the first 20 days after award, the Contractor shall implement all RAP Intake Process 
components associated with PWS subtasks 5.3 through 5.12.  This deliverable establishes the 
standardized, repeatable, and transparent intake process required to manage Government-
provided requirements for existing HELM PL products, ongoing work, and new capabilities 
introduced during the Period of Performance (PoP). 
 
5.2.2.1 Requirements Analysis Proposal (RAP) 
For each intake request, the Contractor shall develop a RAP that includes: 
• The scope of the effort, high level objectives, and functional/nonfunctional requirements. 
• Analyze each request to determine estimated LOE, required skill sets, potential scrum 
team sizing, risk factors, and estimated timeline. 
• Expected labor mix and a burndown plan illustrating resource utilization across the PoP. 
• Conduct interviews, workshops, or follow up sessions with VA requestors and 
stakeholders, as needed. 
• High level schedule, milestones, and major dependencies. 
• Identified risks, impediments, and mitigation recommendations. 
• Required deliverables and applicable HELM PL processes. 
• Establish a single, unified intake workflow for requirements—both new and existing.  
• Receive, capture, and track all intake requests using VA approved tools. 
• Ensure all requests remain visible to HELM PL leadership, CORs, VA PMs, product 
owners, and appropriate VA stakeholders. 
• Coordinate with requestors to clarify scope, dependencies, constraints, and priority 
factors. 
• Identify standardized LOE outputs to support HELM PL governance and prioritization

5.2.3 PROGRAM INCREMENT PLANNING 
The Contractor shall support and deliver HELM PI Quarterly Planning activities to ensure 
alignment of product development objectives with VA strategic priorities.  PI Planning shall be 
conducted in accordance with SAFe principles and HELM governance standards.   
 
The Contractor shall:  
• Facilitate and participate in HELM PI Planning sessions on a quarterly basis. 
• Prepare and deliver all required planning artifacts, including PI Planning Agenda, Feature 
and Capability Backlogs aligned to VA priorities, Capacity Planning for HELM teams 
and resources, Dependency Identification and Resolution Plans across teams and 
programs, and Risk Assessment and Mitigation Strategies documented in a PI Risk 
Register included in PI Planning Summary Report.  
• Coordinate with VA stakeholders to ensure all backlogs (initiatives, epics and stories) are 
prepared and prioritized and organized into Pre-Read Materials demonstrating that 
PL/SPL and Architecture visions are aligned, and Strength, Weakness, Opportunities and 
Threats (SWOT) analysis are completed prior to planning sessions and prepare PI 
Planning Summary Report, to include any objectives. 
• Engage with other contractor teams and enterprise stakeholders during PI Planning to 
ensure shared understanding of dependencies, promote collaboration, and support 
efficient, non-duplicative execution of product development activities. 
• Provide logistical and technical support for PI Planning events (virtual or in-person), 
including scheduling, tools setup (e.g., Jira, Rally, Mural), and facilitation. 
• Provide post-PI planning support including updating the HELM PL Program Backlog and 
Roadmap and providing incremental improvement opportunities for each PI iteration to 
mature the Product Line PI Planning over time. 
• Coordinate with VA Stakeholder to ensure PI Planning Objectives statuses are tracked  
• Coordinate with VA stakeholders quarterly to determine actual completed objectives 
versus planned objectives and provide in a Quarterly PI report.  The report shall provide 
the PI Planned vs Actual completed objectives. 
 
Deliverables: 
A. PI Planning Agenda and Pre-Read Materials 
B. PI Planning Summary Report 
C. HELM PL Program Backlog and HELM Strategic Roadmap 
D. Quarterly PI Report

5.2.4 INNOVATION TREND ANALYSIS AND PLANNING 
The Contractor shall support innovation and modernization efforts by identifying and analyzing 
emerging technologies and industry trends.  The Contractor shall: 
 
• Support HELM PL leadership to create baseline metrics and a roadmap structure for 
digital modernization strategies and to improve modern application architectures.

performance bottlenecks and provide actionable insights, verified by subject matter 
experts, on emerging technologies that can improve system performance, enhance 
automation, and ensure cost efficiency.

potential impact on performance, automation, and operational efficiency. 
• In cooperation with product managers and business stakeholders, identify realistic 
potential use cases for emerging technologies within supply chain.   
• The Contractor shall advise software obsolescence planning for technical refreshes per 
TRM and Digital Transformation Center (DTC). 
 
5.3 SCRUM TEAM SUPPORT (OPTIONAL TASK 1)  
VA may exercise this Optional Task for Scrum Team support, at any time and from time to time 
within the base and/or each option period, to support HELM PL products and projects with the 
continuous delivery pipelines as described in PWS sections 5.5 through 5.12.  Each exercise may 
encompass more than one product or project area.  The Contractor shall provide Agile Scrum 
Teams to support VA Products delivery, including development, testing, integration, data 
activities, configuration, training, sustainment, and modernization.  These teams shall follow 
SAFe Agile, DevSecOps, and HELM PL governance practices and shall meet or exceed maturity 
expectations for iterative delivery.   
 
These teams shall provide comprehensive end-to-end support across the SDLC which may 
include: 
 
• Backlog refinement, elaboration, and decomposition into Features and User Stories 
• Sprint Planning, execution, reviews, and retrospectives 
• Building, testing, and delivering working software increments 
• Supporting DevSecOps pipelines and automation 
• Integrating with dependent solutions and enterprise services 
• Providing Tier II/III troubleshooting and sustainment support 
• Supporting PI Planning and Product Line roadmap execution 
• Ensuring delivery of high-quality, traceable, and testable work products  
• Enabling accurate measurement of business value, throughput, and delivery efficiency   
• O&M sustainment services 
• Technical Support  
 
Scrum Team Type and Sizes:  
Scrum Teams shall be activated only when exercised by the Government via a contract 
modification. See the HELM Intake Process, see PWS paragraph 5.2.2.  A Full-Time Equivalent 
is considered 1920 hrs. per 12 months. All team types follow the same activation rules, reporting 

--- PAGE 50 ---
36C10B26R0048 
 
 
Page 50 of 153 
requirements, and SAFe compliance. The specific scope of functionality to be delivered for each 
build in the optional task shall be determined through requirements submitted through the Intake 
Process. Teams shall be priced for a monthly increment with PoPs ranging from one to twelve 
months and shall coincide with the period in which it is exercised. Each Team exercised shall not 
exceed 12 months or the period in which it is exercised. HELM PMs prior to start of a build 
cycle when applicable shall approve based on current business priorities.

5.5.1 SOFTWARE DEVELOPMENT LIFECYCLE ACTIVITIES 
The Contractor shall provide a hybrid SDLC approach that integrates Agile iterative delivery 
with the documentation, governance, compliance, and quality controls required by VA. The 
Contractor shall continuously support the iterative build and development methodology to 
complete all epics and user stories identified in the backlog and shall ensure that delivery 
practices remain consistent with the SDLC approach defined within PWS Section 5.5 and all 
related subsections. Prioritization shall be informed by discovery activities, stakeholder input, 
operational needs, and product Key Performance Indicators/Objectives and Key Results 
(KPIs/OKRs). KPIs/OKRs shall be developed in coordination with the VA Product Owner and 
stakeholders and shall include methodologies that enable objective, lifecycle long evaluation of 
product performance and user outcomes. The hybrid model shall enable evolutionary (iterative 
and incremental) product development that produces high‑quality outcomes in a cost‑effective, 
timely, and collaborative manner. This requires open communication and coordination across all 
participants contributing to a project, program, or portfolio with multiple stakeholders and 
consumers. 
 
The Contractor shall design, configure, develop, modernize, enhance, maintain, test, and deploy 
builds of each product on a recurring basis no shorter than two weeks and no longer than three 
months throughout the Period of Performance. The Contractor shall provide all deliverables 
Build and Deployment Packages and artifacts specified herein that satisfy VA documentation, 
compliance, and code delivery requirements, and shall ensure these artifacts conform to 
applicable VA policies and enterprise standards.  The Contractor shall leverage and reuse 
relevant content and existing documentation where appropriate to meet the expectations of 
required package deliverables. 
 
The Contractor shall utilize DevSecOps principles, hybrid SDLC practices, and SAFe 
frameworks in partnership with VA to further mature the HELM PL. The Contractor shall follow 
an agile process model for the development of HELM capabilities, including ongoing 

--- PAGE 53 ---
36C10B26R0048 
 
 
Page 53 of 153 
requirements, elaboration and decomposition, iterative design and solutioning, and continuous 
integration of working software. The Contractor shall deliver, when applicable, an Updated 
Technical Design and Product Schedule and Roadmap for each product when applicable. The 
Contractor shall ensure that all SDLC activities align with evolving VA governance processes, 
security controls, and enterprise architecture methodologies, allowing the SDLC approach to 
adapt as VA standards and toolsets mature over the life of the task order. 
 
The Contractor shall produce all required SDLC deliverables in accordance with VA standards, 
including but not limited to Sprint/Iteration Planning Artifacts, Build and Deployment Packages, 
Compliance Documentation, Updated Technical Designs, Product Schedules/Roadmaps, and 
Product Plans/Modernization Roadmaps with associated KPIs/OKRs. All deliverables shall be 
maintained as current, review ready artifacts that support VA governance, traceability, and 
oversight throughout the Period of Performance. 
 
Deliverables: 
A. Sprint/Iteration Planning Artifacts 
B. Build and Deployment Packages

5.5.2 CONFIGURATION MANAGEMENT 
The Contractor shall:  
• Comply with the existing Configuration Management plans in place and offer procedural 
improvements to VA to mature existing processes. 
• Identify the standard and unique aspects of configuration management to be performed 
for each Agile Product Team (APT) by establishing a Product Configuration 
Management (CM) Plan (PCMP) which meets OIT VA CM plan requirements.  The 
Contractor shall reflect all CM required activities and standards in each product-level CM 
plan while determining the unique aspects of the product which require individualized 
procedures.  The CORs and VA PM shall approve the PCMP.  
• Deliver a Recommended List of Configuration Items as part of the PCMP to be placed 
under configuration and change control. The Contractor shall identify types of 
configuration items pertaining to each product to be placed under configuration 
management. Based on Health Portfolio requirements, and the unique needs or nature of 
each product, the Contractor shall determine the components within each product that 
must be under configuration control.  
• Use a VA-approved tool and repository for all software source code and electronic 
artifact configuration and version management. The Contractor shall use the VA-
approved tool to manage change, activity, issue, action, risk, and other product data as 
prescribed by VA standards and processes. If assigned a product using tools that are 
being decommissioned the Contractor shall assist the VA Tools Team in migrating 
products using other Change and/or Configuration Management tools.  
• Ensure that all product software and non-software artifacts are versioned correctly 
according to VA standards and follow a build/release promotion versioning approach 
which identifies all major and minor update changes to the components.  
• Create Project and Product Artifacts baselined and versioned in the CM repository to 
allow the tool to show active and past histories of the check-ins and check-outs of all

5.5.3 RISK & ISSUE MANAGEMENT 
The Contractor shall perform risk management for all work conducted under this task order and 
shall enter risk information into the Product Risk Management Registry using the VA approved 
toolset the VA Product (Line) Accountability and Reporting System (VA PARS) Dashboard, 
JIRA/GITHUB and/or other OIT approved tools maintained by the VA. 
 
The Contractor shall: 
• Comply with the existing Risk Management Plan (RMP)  by the VA. 
• Use the VA approved tool to create, update, and actively manage a project risk register 
(risk log) that identifies potential circumstances that may negatively impact project 
success, describes the possible impacts, and documents actions to reduce or eliminate 
those impacts. Critical risks, as determined by the VA PM, shall also be recorded in the 
VA PARS Dashboard, JIRA/GITHUB and/or other OIT approved tools. 
• Use the VA approved tool to support and facilitate risk meetings and reviews. 
• Actively manage risks and issues, determine level of effort, develop approved mitigations 
and Plans of Action and Milestones (POA&M), and assess or reassess probability and 
severity throughout the project lifecycle. 
• Assess the status of assigned risks and provide a weekly Risk Management Status Report 
to the VA for inclusion in the broader Risk Management Registry. When new risks arise 
that could affect development, testing, or deployment schedules, the Contractor shall 
notify the CORs and VA PM via email within 24 hours.  The email subject line shall 
state: “{Project Name} Risk Alert Notification." 
 
5.5.4 SCHEDULE MANAGEMENT  
The Contractor shall create, maintain, analyze, and report individual product Roadmaps and 
Release Train Schedules in the VA-approved toolset.  The Contractor shall provide schedule 
updates within the VA-approved toolset on a weekly basis.  The Contractor shall evolve the 
roadmap, iteratively maturing. HELM PL support shall include management of patches to 
mitigate collisions in addition to the PL tracking.  HELM PL tracking shall be 
planned/coordinated to ensure optimized sequencing of releases to avoid patch collisions, and all 
risks are documented in VA approved toolset within two days of the risk being realized.  
 
The Contractor shall rollup product roadmaps to produce HELM PL Strategic Roadmap.

5.5.5 USER EXPERIENCE MANAGEMENT 
The Contractor shall apply Human Centered Design (HCD) principles across HELM PL 
development, modernization, and sustainment activities to ensure solutions are intuitive, 
accessible, and aligned with end user needs.  All user experience (UX) research, findings, and 
design decisions shall be documented and maintained in VA approved tools to support 
traceability, PL visibility, and continuous improvement. The Contractor shall ensure all UX 
research, insights, artifacts, and design decisions are documented in VA approved tools and 
linked to requirements, backlog items, and PI Planning artifacts to maintain full traceability and 
alignment with HELM PL governance. 
 
The Contractor shall conduct user research to identify and validate the needs, workflows, and 
operational challenges of VA supply chain, logistics, and acquisition stakeholders supported by 
HELM SCM capabilities. Research activities shall gather input from representative user groups 
across VA to inform requirements, process improvements, and solution design. Findings shall be 
incorporated into system development and modernization efforts to ensure capabilities are 
usable, aligned with mission needs, and supportive of VA’s standardized, streamlined, and 
modern enterprise supply chain objectives.  All research shall follow established human centered 
design practices and comply with VA accessibility and usability standards. 
 
The Contractor shall: 
 Conduct user research studies to understand users’ goals, needs, and pain points, applying 
these insights to define product functionality. 
 Engage with product stakeholders to determine the user base, propose survey questions, 
and identify the best methods for survey distribution. 
 Disseminate surveys, collect feedback, and analyze the data to provide insights into how 
products can be updated based on user input. 
 Craft and test design deliverables such as wireframes and prototypes to facilitate usability 
testing and agile development. 
 Document all findings and insights in a User Experience Monthly Report and update the 
product backlog accordingly 
 
Deliverable:  
A. User Experience Report  
 
5.5.6 AGILE REQUIREMENTS ELABORATION 
The Contractor shall complete an initial Backlog Refinement session with the VA team to 
properly understand and elaborate business Agile requirements. The outcome of this session 
shall be a complete review of the initial user stories, consensus among team members, including 
user stories added as a result of Backlog Refinement. This would be achieved by decomposing 
Initiatives into Epics, Epics into Stories and tasks that represent stakeholder needs, business 
requirements, business rules, requirements visualizations and user story elaborations.  Backlog 
Refinement and prioritization with the VA team are continued throughout the product life cycle 
and shall be managed throughout the PoP. The Contractor shall facilitate any stakeholder 
briefings, meetings and/or elicitation sessions.

Grooming effort. 
 Populate the backlog during an initial planning session identifying all features the team

 Establish initial Unit of measurement (e.g. Story Points) as the estimated relative

5.5.8 PRODUCT ARCHITECTURE 
The Contractor shall lead architecture and discovery activities for each assigned HELM product, 
ensuring that solution’s intent, requirements, and technical direction are clearly defined, 
documented, and aligned with PL guidance. The Contractor shall be responsible for translating 
business needs into product‑specific architectural models, epics, features, and stories, while 
managing product‑level risks, dependencies, and design considerations. The Contractor shall 
work directly with VA customers and product stakeholders to understand operational needs, 
prioritize capabilities, establish the product vision and roadmap, and ensure that the product’s 
technical architecture supports both near‑term delivery and long‑term modernization objectives.   
 
The Contractor shall maintain accurate and current architecture artifacts and guide work through 
the product team as described below. 
 
 Perform product-level discovery and planning for the assigned product, including review 
of existing VA solutions, dependent products, and business processes specific to that 
product. 
 Build and maintain a HELM-wide SAFe Architectural Runway by working with PL 
leadership, solution architects, and dependent teams to define and evolve shared technical 
foundations, architectural enablers, integration points, and cross product capabilities; 
update quarterly prior to each PI. 
 Work closely with VA customers for that product to understand requirements, map needs 
to VA capabilities, and support refinement of epics into features and stories. 
 Develop AS IS Architecture Models for the specific product using VA‑prescribed and 
enterprise‑approved architecture formats (such as DoDAF and UAF), capturing user 
feedback, technical baselines, and product‑specific process flows. 
 Develop TO BE Architecture Models for the product using the current VA‑approved 
enterprise architecture methodology, representing the future-state architecture that aligns 
with Product Line and enterprise direction. 
 Develop and maintain current architecture diagrams (logical, network, and dataflow) for 
the product, update each release and consistent with the enterprise architecture modeling 
approach in use at the time. 
 Develop a Solution‑Level Roadmap for a particular HELM product that provides an 
outline as to how various products, technologies, or systems will be integrated over a 
multi-year horizon. 
 Define solution-level (product-specific) epics with acceptance criteria and ensure 
alignment to product goals and Product Line architecture. 
 Develop the Solution Architecture for the product, including technical, business, and data 
models/components, in accordance with the architectural modeling standards and 
practices adopted by VA during the period of performance. 
 Support early user interface and technical prototypes for the product. 

--- PAGE 58 ---
36C10B26R0048 
 
 
Page 58 of 153 
 Support business process modeling for product workflows. 
 Guide product team execution by maintaining requirements, updating solution intent, and 
ensuring alignment to planned KPIs during PI execution. 
 
Deliverables: 
A. Product Line SAFe Architectural Runway 
B. AS IS Architecture Model  
C. TO BE Architecture Model  
D. Architecture Diagrams  
E. Solution‑Level Roadmap (product‑specific) 
F. Solution Architecture (product-specific) 
G. Product‑Specific Data Models 
H. User Research Artifacts 
I. Solution (Product) Roadmap 
 
5.5.9 PRODUCT TESTING 
The Contractor shall perform product‑specific testing activities in alignment with the hybrid 
SDLC model, Agile development practices, and continuous delivery approach described in this 
PWS.  The Contractor shall conduct testing on a recurring, iterative basis to validate 
functionality, performance, accessibility, security, and interoperability of each product 
throughout the development lifecycle.  Testing shall be integrated into every sprint and release 
cycle and shall comply with VA policy, VA governance processes, and VA‑approved testing 
requirements, including Health Portfolio Jira Management Requirements where applicable. 
 
The Contractor shall design, implement, and maintain automated test scripts wherever feasible to 
improve coverage, reduce manual effort, and support consistent, repeatable validation of product 
functionality. Automated regression testing and continuous integration capabilities shall be 
leveraged using VA TRM‑approved tools.  The Contractor shall support manual testing where 
necessary for specialized validation or compliance activities. 
 
The Contractor shall work collaboratively with product teams, VA stakeholders, and dependent 
solution groups to define test conditions, maintain alignment with evolving requirements, 
validate acceptance criteria, and ensure readiness for release.  The Contractor shall support 
continuous improvement by refining test approaches, expanding automation capabilities, and 
improving test quality over the Period of Performance. 
 
5.5.9.1 Integrated Testing Activities 
The Contractor shall plan, execute, document, and maintain testing activities across all phases of 
the system lifecycle, including user story, sprint, build, release, and production readiness testing.   
 
At a minimum, testing shall include: 
 Unit testing 
 Functional testing 
 Accessibility testing (Section 508) 
 System testing 
 Reliability testing 
 Usability testing 
 Interoperability testing 

--- PAGE 59 ---
36C10B26R0048 
 
 
Page 59 of 153 
 Regression testing (automated where feasible) 
 Security testing 
 Performance and load testing 
 
The Contractor shall conduct testing of non‑functional requirements, including installation, 
configuration, backout, rollback, failover, and recovery procedures. Testing shall utilize 
continuous integration and automated regression capabilities using VA TRM‑approved tools, 
such as Selenium, JUnit, NUnit, Jenkins, GitLab CI/CD, Cucumber, Postman, and Cypress, 
provided they are approved at the time of use. 
 
The Contractor shall participate in and support enterprise and compliance testing activities, 
including: 
 Security assessments 
 Accessibility compliance reviews 
 Performance and load testing events 
 Technical standards validation 
 Architectural compliance reviews 
 User Acceptance Testing (UAT) 
 Initial Operational Capability (IOC) validation 
 
Security scanning shall be performed using VA‑approved tools, including Web Application 
Security Assessments (WASA), static code analysis (e.g., Fortify), and dependency/vulnerability 
scanning solutions.  The Contractor shall remediate identified vulnerabilities in accordance with 
VA‑defined severity timelines. 
 
5.5.9.2 Test Planning and Documentation 
The Contractor shall: 
 Develop and maintain a Test Plan within the VA Quality Manager tool. 
 Provide complete Test Strategy and Test Execution content using VA‑provided 
templates. 
 Enter all test cases, test scripts (automated and manual), test data, execution results, and 
supporting artifacts into the VA‑approved tool. 
 Maintain and update the Requirements Traceability Matrix (RTM) to ensure 
bi‑directional traceability between requirements, test cases, defects, and releases. 
 Update all testing artifacts daily to ensure visibility and accuracy of status. 
 Place all testing artifacts under version control in a VA‑approved repository. 
 Develop and deliver a Requirements Traceability Matrix (RTM) that documents and 
maps all requirements to corresponding design elements, test cases, and validation 
evidence, and shall update the RTM throughout the period of performance to ensure 
complete traceability. 
 
 
5.5.9.3 Defect Management 
The Contractor shall: 
 Log all defects in the VA‑approved tool within one business day of identification. 
 Assign severity and impact levels consistent with VA‑defined criteria. 
 Support the VA PM and CORs in defect prioritization and backlog grooming. 

--- PAGE 60 ---
36C10B26R0048 
 
 
Page 60 of 153 
 Track defects through resolution, retesting, and closure. 
 
Defects shall be remediated within the following maximum timeframes: 
 Critical – 5 business days 
 High – 10 business days 
 Medium – 30 business days 
 Low – As prioritized by the VA PM 
 
Defects shall not be closed without documented validation evidence and successful retesting. 
 
5.5.9.4 Reporting 
The Contractor shall provide: 
 Test execution status reporting per sprint and per release. 
 A Test Summary Report at the conclusion of each release (or sprint if required). 
 Performance and load testing reports. 
 Security scan results and documented remediation evidence. 
 Daily defect status updates. 
 
All assessment results from Contractor‑led and VA‑led reviews shall be consolidated in the 
VA‑approved tool for reporting and audit purposes. 
 
5.5.9.5 Release Readiness Criteria 
Prior to production deployment, the Contractor shall: 
 Execute no less than ninety‑five percent (95%) of planned test cases for the release. 
 Ensure all Critical and High defects are resolved or formally accepted by the VA PM. 
 Ensure zero (0) open Critical or High security vulnerabilities unless risk acceptance is 
formally approved by the Authorizing Official (AO). 
 Obtain documented VA PM approval to proceed to production. 
 
Deliverables: 
A. Test Plans 
B. Test Cases 
C. Test Scripts (including automated test scripts where applicable) 
D. Test Execution & Summary Reports 
E. Defect Management Reports   
 
F. Requirements Traceability Matrix (RTM)  
G. Test Summary Report(s) (per sprint and/or release as required) 
 
5.5.10 DATA MANAGEMENT AND MIGRATION SUPPORT 
The Contractor shall provide support for database solutions and design strategies for data 
migration during VA site implementations, and shall supply resources for site data mapping, 
extraction, transformation, migration, analytics, and validation from VA legacy systems (e.g., 
AEMS/MERS, GIP, Maximo) to modernize  systems as required. Data may need to be delivered 
to third parties (e.g., MEDLOG) in accordance with the standards provided.  The Contractor 
shall comply with PWS paragraph 5.5.1 SDLC Activities for all data migration development 
efforts and shall adhere to applicable government and VA standards, including security and 
organizational controls for scripts, tools, and artifacts.

5.5.10.1 Data Tools Development and Testing Support 
The Contractor shall design, develop, test, update, and manage tools to obtain and manage data 
sets from disparate sources, including subject matter expert input.  The Contractor shall provide 
project management plans for these tool development efforts and be accountable to cost and 
schedule; tool development and updates shall follow VA TRM‑approved technologies and 
practices, as applicable. 
 
Key activities include: 
• Developing and testing data tools to obtain and manage data sets from various sources. 
• Managing updates to tools and supporting ongoing operations aligned to the project 
management plan. 
• Providing project management plans and periodic updates that govern scope, schedule, 
and cost for data tool development and testing. 
 
5.5.10.2 Data Extraction and Analytics 
The Contractor shall perform mapping, extraction, transformation, migration, and analytics for 
data from disparate legacy sources, and provide analytical support to establish and refine 
extraction, transformation, and migration standards and processes.  The Contractor shall 
troubleshoot and resolve identified data issues, and provide periodic input to updates and/or 
assist in establishing the Data Migration Plan (DMP), including schedules where one is not 
already established by the VA.  The Contractor shall adhere to government standards where 
applicable for the security and organization of extraction and transformation scripts. 
 
Key activities include: 
• Developing, organizing, and maintaining data extraction scripts. 
• Establishing and updating the Data Migration Plan and schedule (as applicable). 
• Delivering mapped and transformed data to VA systems and approved third parties (e.g., 
MEDLOG) per provided standards. 
 
5.5.10.3 Data Mapping and Validation 
The Contractor shall perform comprehensive data mapping analysis to ensure data from legacy 
systems is identified, cleansed, enriched, and structured appropriately for migration.  The 
Contractor shall develop and provide a Data Mapping and Validation Plan, execute validation 
activities, analyze results, and identify corrections for any data issues.  A Data Mapping 
Document shall be maintained to reflect the authoritative mapping rules, transformations, and 
quality checks for each migration. 
 
Key activities include: 
• Producing and maintaining a Data Mapping and Validation Plan. 
• Conducting data profiling, cleansing, enrichment, and validation aligned to VA standards. 
• Generating Data Mapping Analysis and Validation Results and implementing corrective 
actions. 
 
5.5.10.4 Migration Planning, Environment Support, and Testing 
The Contractor shall support the migration of products between environments (e.g., 
development, test, staging, production) by coordinating with infrastructure points of contact and 
providing technical support for testing, configuration, and documentation.  The Contractor shall

• Providing technical support for migration testing, configuration, and documentation. 
• Developing and maintaining migration planning and testing artifacts that confirm 
readiness and quality.

existing users.

instructor led virtual classes, webinars, demonstrations, and interactive training modules 
to support onboarding and ongoing education for HELM Product Line users.  
• The Contractor shall provide recordings of training webinars.  
• Create Virtual Training Materials and Artifacts - Develop and maintain user focused 
training materials, including presentations, guides, reference documents, job aids, videos, 
and self-paced learning content. All materials shall be stored in VA approved 
repositories.  
• Support New and Existing Users - Deliver training designed for both new users requiring 
introductory instruction and existing users requiring refresher training or updated 
instruction for new functionality or existing functionality due to bug fixes, enhancements, 
or procedural changes. 
• Provide Post Training Support - Offer follow up sessions, office hours support, and Q&A 
opportunities to ensure users can effectively apply learned content and incorporate 
updated processes related to HELM Product Line solutions.

5.5.12 AI/ML OPTIMIZATION AND PREDICTIVE ANALYTICS 
The Contractor shall incorporate artificial intelligence and machine learning (AI/ML) capabilities 
for HELM PL to optimize products and reduce operational risks. 
 
 The Contractor shall: 
 Implement AI/ML algorithms to enhance predictive forecasting and provide near real-

 Develop automated capabilities for the identification and validation of product 
substitutions during supply disruptions to maintain continuity of care.  Provide a

 Provide actionable analytics to empower stakeholders with information needed for 
continuous improvement and operational excellence. 
 Provide a Product Substitution Automation Guide to describe how product substitutions

5.6 RELEASE SUPPORT  
The activities in this product/project area are applicable to Optional Task One Scrum Team 
Support requirements at PWS Section 5.3 and Optional Task Two Highly Technical Scrum Team 
Support requirements at PWS Section 5.4. 
 
The Contractor shall support the VA DevSecOps Release Process.  The Release Process governs 
deployment of new functionality, enhancements, and defect remediation in alignment with the 
VA DevSecOps Release Framework.  The Contractor shall use the VA approved release 
calendaring process and toolset (e.g., ServiceNow) to track release activities, dependencies, 
special works in progress, and production deployment events.

The Contractor shall provide all required information to populate and maintain ServiceNow 
release calendar entries for each release. 
 
The DevSecOps Release Process includes pre-release and release activities and may require 
coordination with an appropriate Release Train Engineer (RTE) Lead. 
 
5.6.1 PRERELEASE SUPPORT  
The Contractor shall support the HELM PL Release Process throughout the build lifecycle, 
ensuring alignment with HELM Agile, DevSecOps, and CI/CD workflows.  VA Release Agents 
shall review artifacts for completeness, traceability, and compliance with Release Process 
standards.  The Contractor shall integrate secure code scanning, CI/CD pipelines, configuration 
validation, dependency inventories, and artifact promotion controls.  
 
The Contractor shall prepare the Build Release Package, including: 
• Production Operations Manual (POM) / Technical Manual (workflows, RACI, 
dependencies, monitoring indicators)  
• User Guide for business and HELM PL users  
• Version Description Document (VDD) (configuration, traceability, audit support)  
• Transition Plan (architecture, interfaces, environments, pipeline dependencies)  
• Deployment & Installation Guide (deployment patterns, rollback, contingency, Disaster 
Recovery (DR), security keys)  
• All ATO related documentation, including RMF, scanning evidence, and continuous 
monitoring items.  
• The Contractor shall collaborate with the VA PM, HELM Leadership, and stakeholders 
to develop a comprehensive Build Release Package that satisfies all HELM release 
readiness, deployment, and governance requirements. 
• The VA assigned contractor Release Agents shall perform ongoing reviews of HELM 
product artifacts within VA mandated repositories and provide feedback on 
documentation completeness, traceability, and compliance with Release Process 
standards. 
• The Contractor shall ensure that code commits, test results, configuration items, 
dependency inventories, and deployment artifacts meet HELM quality gates and are 
properly linked in HELM repositories, backlogs, and pipeline orchestration tools. 
• Pre-Release Support shall integrate HELM’s DevSecOps toolchain, including automated 
CI/CD pipelines, secure code scanning, configuration validation, and artifact promotion 
controls.   
 
All prerelease activities shall comply with HELM PL governance, VA Agile/DevSecOps 
policies, VA security and quality standards, and CI/CD integration requirements. 
 
Deliverables: 
A. Build Release Package

5.6.3 INITIAL OPERATING CAPABILITY SUPPORT  
The Contractor shall support the Initial Operating Capability (IOC) evaluation for HELM PL 
product line, including Asset Management, Inventory Management, Prosthetics, and other supply 
chain modernization solutions.  IOC is a limited production release conducted after all testing 
has been completed and user acceptance has been granted. During IOC, the Contractor shall 
assist HELM PL by tracking issues, investigating defects, supporting remediation activities, and 
ensuring that IOC entry and exit criteria are met for each applicable HELM product. 
 
The Contractor shall support a 10-day errorfree IOC period in the production environment.  All 
issues and defects identified during IOC shall be documented in the Defect/Fix Status Report. 
For each defect, the Contractor shall prepare an IOC Defect Resolution Plan that identifies the 
corrective action, required updates to software code or documentation, and impacts to schedule. 
Upon CORs approval, the Contractor shall execute the approved plan and deliver updated 
software source code, compiled code, and supporting documentation. 
 
Following any defect correction, the IOC period shall continue for a minimum of five 
consecutive error free business days before IOC may be exited.  The Contractor shall coordinate 
installation and configuration of updates at VA test sites and support validation of fixes across 
the relevant HELM product lines. 
 

--- PAGE 66 ---
36C10B26R0048 
 
 
Page 66 of 153 
The Contractor shall support all IOC entry and exit activities and shall prepare and deliver an 
IOC Entry Request and final IOC Exit Summary documenting completion of all requirements 
and readiness for full deployment. 
 
Deliverables: 
A. Defect/Fix Status Report   
B. IOC Defect Resolution Plan  
C. Updated Software Source Code, Compiled Code, and Supporting Documentation  
D. IOC Entry Request and IOC Exit Summary 
 
5.6.4 RELEASE AND DEPLOYMENT SUPPORT 
The Contractor shall support both release activities (preparing, documenting, packaging, and 
obtaining required approvals for a software version) and deployment activities (executing the 
movement of that version into production).  The Contractor shall ensure all Pre‑Release, A&A, 
and IOC criteria have been met before deployment occurs.  The Contractor shall coordinate each 
release with VA technical staff, integration partners, and teams supporting interfacing systems to 
resolve issues and ensure all required components are migrated to the appropriate technical 
environments as planned. 
 
The Contractor shall participate in release and deployment planning and coordination meetings, 
verify that deployment checklists, prerequisites, and dependencies are complete, and confirm 
readiness for production migration.  The Contractor shall prepare and maintain all documentation 
and artifacts that make up the Release and Deployment Support Package, which includes, at a 
minimum: 
 
• Solution Deployment Package (SDP), including smart scripting and all required artifacts 
covering Release Management, Product Documentation, and Product Support phases 
• Updated Build Release Package reflecting final build and deployment changes 
• Release Notes describing new features, fixes, and configuration changes 
• Updated design documents 
• Final source code 
• Evidence of compliance with the VA DevSecOps Release Framework 
• Final technical and operational documentation reflecting any changes during IOC or 
production deployment 
 
The Contractor shall also define the processes, coordination activities, responsibilities, and 
overall approach for carrying out all release and deployment tasks in the Release and 
Deployment Support Plan. 
 
The Contractor shall ensure these materials remain current and are recorded using VA‑approved 
tools that support documentation, workflows, and release tracking.  To support the complete 
lifecycle of preparing and delivering a release into production, the Contractor shall perform 
activities that span planning and readiness, deployment execution, post‑deployment monitoring, 
and release closure.  
 
The following subsections define the major activity areas required to ensure each release is 
prepared, deployed, validated, and completed in a controlled and coordinated manner.
